I’ve been getting through things, I guess, but I wouldn’t say I’m energized or anything like that.\n\nI’m sorry, is that the kind of thing you mean? Like feeling wired? Because I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to sound dramatic… but yeah, kind of.\n\nI keep thinking the supervisors are changing my shifts on purpose, and watching me on the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or something. People say it’s just staffing shortages, and I try to believe that, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to accuse anyone, I just… that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I don’t think so.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a noise or someone talking nearby, but when I check there’s usually some explanation, or I’m just tired. I wouldn’t say I’m hearing voices when no one’s around. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling emotionally flat, like your face or voice doesn’t show much feeling even when something matters to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think so… or at least I haven’t noticed it.\n\nPeople haven’t said I look blank or anything. I mean, I can be quiet, especially when I’m tired or stressed, but I wouldn’t say I don’t feel things. Maybe I don’t always show it right? I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days I wake up already feeling really heavy, like empty inside.\n\nAnd after work sometimes I’ll just sit in my car for a while and… cry before I can even drive home. I know that probably sounds like a lot. But yeah, it’s been most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that feel very disorganized or that other people have said seem unusual or hard to understand?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything really bizarre, because I can usually do my job, but there have been moments at work where I sort of… get stuck.\n\nLike I’ve caught myself rocking in the hallway, or copying a patient’s tapping without really meaning to. And once or twice I’ll be standing there with my gloves only halfway on, just kind of frozen, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound good. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything… it just happens sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you still felt watched or suspicious even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was a time like that.\n\nI wasn’t, like, in the really deep depressed place for a few weeks, or at least not as bad. But I still felt like the cameras at work were watching me more than they should be, and like the supervisors were kind of monitoring what I was doing.\n\nI tried to just act normal, because I know that sounds… paranoid or something. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems with suspiciousness and your speech being hard to keep on track been going on for more than one month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been more than a month. I guess I was hoping it was just a phase or stress or something, but if I’m being honest, the feeling watched stuff and getting kind of mixed up or stuck has been going on for… about a year. Maybe a year and a month.\n\nI don’t mean it’s been every single second, but it’s been around that long, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you still felt watched or suspicious even though you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I wasn’t in that really deep depressed place for a few weeks, so I guess that would count.\n\nBut I still had the feeling that the cameras at work were kind of focused on me, and that supervisors were watching or tracking what I was doing. I tried not to make a big deal out of it, and I kept telling myself to just act normal, but I was still guarded there.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad… but yes, there was a stretch like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost motivation to do everyday things, even basic tasks, more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, more than I want to admit.\n\nI used to do stuff without really thinking about it—like sketching mural ideas, cooking for friends, playing soccer on weekends.
